\"\"\"\n        Applies a function to every dataset in one or more DataTree objects with\n        the same structure (ie.., that are isomorphic), returning new trees which\n        store the results.\n    \n        The function will be applied to any dataset stored in any of the nodes in\n        the trees. The returned trees will have the same structure as the supplied\n        trees.\n    \n        ``func`` needs to return a Dataset, tuple of Dataset objects or None in order\n        to be able to rebuild the subtrees after mapping, as each result will be\n        assigned to its respective node of a new tree via `DataTree.from_dict`. Any\n        returned value that is one of these types will be stacked into a separate\n        tree before returning all of them.\n    \n        ``map_over_datasets`` is essentially syntactic sugar for the combination of\n        ``group_subtrees`` and ``DataTree.from_dict``. For example, in the case of\n        a two argument function that return one result, it is equivalent to::\n    \n            results = {}\n            for path, (left, right) in group_subtrees(left_tree, right_tree):\n                results[path] = func(left.dataset, right.dataset)\n            return DataTree.from_dict(results)\n    \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        func : callable\n            Function to apply to datasets with signature:\n    \n            `func(*args: Dataset, **kwargs) -> Union[Dataset, tuple[Dataset, ...]]`.\n    \n            (i.e. func must accept at least one Dataset and return at least one Dataset.)\n        *args : tuple, optional\n            Positional arguments passed on to `func`. The returned trees will have the same structure as the supplied\n        trees.\n    \n        ``func`` needs to return a Dataset, tuple of Dataset objects or None in order\n        to be able to rebuild the subtrees after mapping, as each result will be\n        assigned to its respective node of a new tree via `DataTree.from_dict`. For example, in the case of\n        a two argument function that return one result, it is equivalent to::\n    \n            results = {}\n            for path, (left, right) in group_subtrees(left_tree, right_tree):\n                results[path] = func(left.dataset, right.dataset)\n            return DataTree.from_dict(results)\n    \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        func : callable\n            Function to apply to datasets with signature:\n    \n            `func(*args: Dataset, **kwargs) -> Union[Dataset, tuple[Dataset, ...]]`.\n    \n            (i.e. func must accept at least one Dataset and return at least one Dataset.)\n        *args : tuple, optional\n            Positional arguments passed on to `func`. ## 71. assistant / message

```
The first test pass exposed two repository integration gaps alongside the core implementation: the stripped function imports in four consumers, and mutation during child detachment. I’m correcting those, plus narrowing `allow_overwrite=False` to existing child nodes (so `DataTree` variable-name collisions retain their established error).
```

```
Implemented TreeNode operations and subtree grouping in `/testbed`.

Highlights:

- Unix-style absolute/relative path lookup and assignment
- Parent reassignment, orphaning, and cycle prevention
- Ancestor, sibling, level, depth, width, leaf, and lineage properties
- Breadth-first subtree traversal with paths
- Lazy `group_subtrees` / `zip_subtrees` isomorphism validation
- Restored integration imports throughout xarray
- Added child mutation hooks and validation

Validation:

- `174 passed, 6 xfailed` across DataTree and mapping tests
- Ruff checks passed
- Broader backend tests passed except unrelated HDF5/h5py runtime failures.
```
